I don't confront them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever avoided leaving your home because you thought there might be spiders outside?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's mostly just... the basement and the shed. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF40.1, F40.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well... I suppose things have just gotten a bit difficult at home. I keep arranging my house around, um... not seeing spiders. \n\nLike, I put off doing the laundry because it's down in the basement. And I completely avoid the garden shed. I've even started paying people to do small jobs around the house that I know perfectly well how to do myself... just because I can’t make myself go into those spaces.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things, like arranging your house or avoiding certain spaces, or is it more that you just prefer to?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said, I really can't make myself go into those spaces. It doesn't exactly feel like a preference. I just... I avoid them because I feel like I have to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel the need to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... check things like that. I just stay away from where they might be.\n\nDoctor: What is it about those spaces that you're afraid of?\n\nPatient: Well... it's just... So I just don't go.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically about being bitten or harmed by spiders, or is it more about the spiders themselves?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't... I don't think about them biting me, really. It's just... the spiders themselves. I just can't be around them.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of all spiders or just certain types of spiders?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really stick around to find out what kind they are. I just... if it's a spider, I want to be as far away from it as possible. I don't think it matters what type it is.\n\nDoctor: How much of your daily life would you say is affected by your efforts to avoid spiders?\n\nPatient: Well... it affects things. Um... it's just harder to keep up with the house. I end up paying people to do small maintenance things that I could probably do myself, if I... if I could go into the basement or the shed. And chores back up. I just... I avoid the parts of my own property that I need to use, really. It just makes things more expensive and... difficult to manage.\n\nDoctor: How would you describe the anxiety you feel when you think about spiders or being in a place where they might be?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I know it probably isn't going to hurt me. Like, a spider just sitting in the corner of an old house. But... if I even picture one dropping out of a box, or... or sitting down near the washer... it just feels... it feels enormous. And really threatening. It's... honestly, it's a bit embarrassing to admit how bad it feels. I'd just rather not think about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were able to confront or be near spiders without avoiding them, or has this avoidance been consistent over time?\n\nPatient: No... not really. It's been pretty consistent. Like I was saying, that's really what brings me in. I just... I keep arranging my own house around not seeing them. I don't confront them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever avoided leaving your home because you thought there might be spiders outside?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's mostly just... the basement and the shed. I still go to work and everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your fear of spiders has prevented you from doing something important or enjoyable outside of your home?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still go out and do things. It's just... mostly certain places, like I said. I haven't missed anything big because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your fear of spiders has interfered with your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: No, not my relationships. My friends and family don't really... it doesn't affect them. It's more just the house. Like I mentioned, it's just getting harder to manage things here. I'm paying people to do basic maintenance because I won't go in the shed, and the laundry piles up. It just makes owning this place more expensive and... frustrating. But it doesn't really come between me and other people.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of spiders has caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word distress. It's just... an annoyance. Like I said, it's frustrating to deal with the house stuff, but... I wouldn't say I'm distressed. I just don't like them.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your efforts to avoid spiders have caused you any significant problems in your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying... it's really just the house. It's gotten harder to manage. I end up paying for help with small maintenance things, or... letting the chores back up because I avoid certain parts of the property. I keep arranging my house around, um... not seeing spiders. \n\nLike, I put off doing the laundry because it's down in the basement. And I completely avoid the garden shed. I've even started paying people to do small jobs around the house that I know perfectly well how to do myself... just because I can’t make myself go into those spaces.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things, like arranging your house or avoiding certain spaces, or is it more that you just prefer to?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said, I really can't make myself go into those spaces. It doesn't exactly feel like a preference. I just... I avoid them because I feel like I have to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel the need to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... check things like that. I just stay away from where they might be.\n\nDoctor: What is it about those spaces that you're afraid of?\n\nPatient: Well... it's just... I know a spider down there probably isn't going to actually hurt me. I'm aware of that. But... if I even think about one, you know, dropping out of a box in the shed, or just sitting there near the washing machine... \n\nIt just feels... threatening. Enormous, almost. It's... a bit embarrassing, really, how much it bothers me. So I just don't go.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically about being bitten or harmed by spiders, or is it more about the spiders themselves?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't... I don't think about them biting me, really. It's just... the spiders themselves. I just can't be around them.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of all spiders or just certain types of spiders?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really stick around to find out what kind they are. I just... if it's a spider, I want to be as far away from it as possible. I don't think it matters what type it is.\n\nDoctor: How much of your daily life would you say is affected by your efforts to avoid spiders?\n\nPatient: Well... it affects things. Um... it's just harder to keep up with the house. I end up paying people to do small maintenance things that I could probably do myself, if I... if I could go into the basement or the shed. And chores back up. I just... I avoid the parts of my own property that I need to use, really. It just makes things more expensive and... difficult to manage.\n\nDoctor: How would you describe the anxiety you feel when you think about spiders or being in a place where they might be?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I'd just rather not think about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were able to confront or be near spiders without avoiding them, or has this avoidance been consistent over time?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't confront them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever avoided leaving your home because you thought there might be spiders outside?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's mostly just... the basement and the shed. I still go to work and everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your fear of spiders has prevented you from doing something important or enjoyable outside of your home?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still go out and do things. It's just... mostly certain places, like I said. I haven't missed anything big because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your fear of spiders has interfered with your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: No, not my relationships. My friends and family don't really... it doesn't affect them. It's more just the house. Like I mentioned, it's just getting harder to manage things here. I just... didn't think it was something you go to a doctor for. I figured it was just something I had to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of spiders only when you're alone or can it happen when you're with other people as well?\n\nPatient: It happens either way. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well... I suppose things have just gotten a bit difficult at home. I keep arranging my house around, um... not seeing spiders. \n\nLike, I put off doing the laundry because it's down in the basement. And I completely avoid the garden shed. I've even started paying people to do small jobs around the house that I know perfectly well how to do myself... just because I can’t make myself go into those spaces.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things, like arranging your house or avoiding certain spaces, or is it more that you just prefer to?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just said, I really can't make myself go into those spaces. It doesn't exactly feel like a preference. I just... I avoid them because I feel like I have to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel the need to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... check things like that. I just stay away from where they might be.\n\nDoctor: What is it about those spaces that you're afraid of?\n\nPatient: Well... it's just... I know a spider down there probably isn't going to actually hurt me. I'm aware of that. But... if I even think about one, you know, dropping out of a box in the shed, or just sitting there near the washing machine... \n\nIt just feels... threatening. Enormous, almost. It's... a bit embarrassing, really, how much it bothers me. So I just don't go.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically about being bitten or harmed by spiders, or is it more about the spiders themselves?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I don't... I don't think about them biting me, really. It's just... the spiders themselves. I just can't be around them.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of all spiders or just certain types of spiders?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really stick around to find out what kind they are. I just... if it's a spider, I want to be as far away from it as possible. I don't think it matters what type it is.\n\nDoctor: How much of your daily life would you say is affected by your efforts to avoid spiders?\n\nPatient: Well... it affects things. Um... it's just harder to keep up with the house. I end up paying people to do small maintenance things that I could probably do myself, if I... if I could go into the basement or the shed. And chores back up. I just... I avoid the parts of my own property that I need to use, really. It just makes things more expensive and... difficult to manage.\n\nDoctor: How would you describe the anxiety you feel when you think about spiders or being in a place where they might be?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I know it probably isn't going to hurt me. Like, a spider just sitting in the corner of an old house. But... if I even picture one dropping out of a box, or... or sitting down near the washer... it just feels... it feels enormous. And really threatening. It's... honestly, it's a bit embarrassing to admit how bad it feels. I'd just rather not think about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were able to confront or be near spiders without avoiding them, or has this avoidance been consistent over time?\n\nPatient: No... not really. It's been pretty consistent. Like I was saying, that's really what brings me in. I just... I keep arranging my own house around not seeing them. Putting off laundry in the basement, avoiding the garden shed... paying people for those small jobs I know perfectly well how to do. I just can't make myself go into those spaces. So... no. I don't confront them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever avoided leaving your home because you thought there might be spiders outside?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's mostly just... the basement and the shed. I still go to work and everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your fear of spiders has prevented you from doing something important or enjoyable outside of your home?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still go out and do things. It's just... mostly certain places, like I said. I haven't missed anything big because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your fear of spiders has interfered with your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: No, not my relationships. My friends and family don't really... it doesn't affect them. It's more just the house. Like I mentioned, it's just getting harder to manage things here. I'm paying people to do basic maintenance because I won't go in the shed, and the laundry piles up. It just makes owning this place more expensive and... frustrating. But it doesn't really come between me and other people.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of spiders has caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word distress. It's just... an annoyance. Like I said, it's frustrating to deal with the house stuff, but... I wouldn't say I'm distressed. I just don't like them.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your efforts to avoid spiders have caused you any significant problems in your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying... it's really just the house. It's gotten harder to manage. I end up paying for help with small maintenance things, or... letting the chores back up because I avoid certain parts of the property. So I guess it makes owning the place a bit more expensive. That's really it, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking help for your fear of spiders before now?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just... didn't think it was something you go to a doctor for. I figured it was just something I had to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of spiders only when you're alone or can it happen when you're with other people as well?\n\nPatient: It happens either way. It doesn't really matter if someone else is there. If I see one... I just react.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of spiders only occur in specific situations, like when you're at home, or can it happen anywhere?\n\nPatient: Anywhere, really. I mean... if I see one, it doesn't matter where I am. The reaction is the same.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. My friends and family don't really... it doesn't affect them. It's more just the house. Like I mentioned, it's just getting harder to manage things here. I'm paying people to do basic maintenance because I won't go in the shed, and the laundry piles up. It just makes owning this place more expensive and... frustrating. It's just... an annoyance. Like I said, it's frustrating to deal with the house stuff, but... I wouldn't say I'm distressed. I just don't like them.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your efforts to avoid spiders have caused you any significant problems in your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying... it's really just the house. It's gotten harder to manage. I end up paying for help with small maintenance things, or... letting the chores back up because I avoid certain parts of the property. And chores back up. I just... I avoid the parts of my own property that I need to use, really. It just makes things more expensive and... difficult to manage.\n\nDoctor: How would you describe the anxiety you feel when you think about spiders or being in a place where they might be?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I know it probably isn't going to hurt me. Like, a spider just sitting in the corner of an old house. But... if I even picture one dropping out of a box, or... or sitting down near the washer... it just feels... it feels enormous. And really threatening. It's... honestly, it's a bit embarrassing to admit how bad it feels. I'd just rather not think about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were able to confront or be near spiders without avoiding them, or has this avoidance been consistent over time?\n\nPatient: No... not really. It's been pretty consistent. Like I was saying, that's really what brings me in. I just... I keep arranging my own house around not seeing them. Putting off laundry in the basement, avoiding the garden shed... paying people for those small jobs I know perfectly well how to do. I just can't make myself go into those spaces. So... no. I don't confront them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever avoided leaving your home because you thought there might be spiders outside?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's mostly just... the basement and the shed. I still go to work and everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your fear of spiders has prevented you from doing something important or enjoyable outside of your home?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still go out and do things. It's just... mostly certain places, like I said. I haven't missed anything big because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your fear of spiders has interfered with your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: No, not my relationships. My friends and family don't really... it doesn't affect them. It's more just the house. Like I mentioned, it's just getting harder to manage things here. I'm paying people to do basic maintenance because I won't go in the shed, and the laundry piles up. It just makes owning this place more expensive and... frustrating. But it doesn't really come between me and other people.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of spiders has caused you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word distress. It's just... an annoyance. Like I said, it's frustrating to deal with the house stuff, but... I wouldn't say I'm distressed. I just don't like them.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your efforts to avoid spiders have caused you any significant problems in your daily routine?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying... it's really just the house. It's gotten harder to manage. I end up paying for help with small maintenance things, or... letting the chores back up because I avoid certain parts of the property. So I guess it makes owning the place a bit more expensive. That's really it, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking help for your fear of spiders before now?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just... didn't think it was something you go to a doctor for. I figured it was just something I had to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of spiders only when you're alone or can it happen when you're with other people as well?\n\nPatient: It happens either way. It doesn't really matter if someone else is there. If I see one... I just react.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of spiders only occur in specific situations, like when you're at home, or can it happen anywhere?\n\nPatient: Anywhere, really. I mean... if I see one, it doesn't matter where I am. The reaction is the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of spiders is greater than the actual danger they pose to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean... I know a spider in the corner of an old house probably isn't going to hurt me. But... if I just picture one dropping out of a box, or sitting near the washer... it just feels enormous to me. And threatening. It's... a bit embarrassing to admit, honestly. But yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social activities because of your fear of possibly encountering spiders?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I still go out and see people. It hasn't really affected that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I just react.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of spiders only occur in specific situations, like when you're at home, or can it happen anywhere?\n\nPatient: Anywhere, really. I mean... if I see one, it doesn't matter where I am. The reaction is the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of spiders is greater than the actual danger they pose to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean... I know a spider in the corner of an old house probably isn't going to hurt me. But... if I just picture one dropping out of a box, or sitting near the washer... it just feels enormous to me. And threatening. It's... a bit embarrassing to admit, honestly. But yes.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social activities because of your fear of possibly encountering spiders?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I still go out and see people. It hasn't really affected that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of spiders triggered by actually seeing a spider, or also by the possibility that one might be present, even if you don't see it?\n\nPatient: Mostly... just if I actually spot one. When that happens, my chest tightens right away. My hands start shaking. I'll either... freeze in the doorway, or I just back out of there so fast. It's like my body just decides to move before I can even think about it."
